 Informally speaking , Prime number theorem states that if a random integer is selected in the range of zero to some large integer N , the probability that the selected integer is prime is about 1 / ln ( N ) , where ln ( N ) is the natural logarithm of N. Consequently , a random integer with at most 2n digits ( for large enough n ) is about half as likely to be prime as a random integer with at most n digits .
 a random integer   is selected
